TROY SCHOOL DISTRICT 
TROY BOARD OF EDUCATION 
TROY, MICHIGAN 
TO: Board of Education 
FROM:  Richard M. Machesky, Ed.D. 
RE: Agenda Memo for the Organizational Meeting, January 15, 2019 
1. ELECTION OF ACTING CHAIRPERSON 
The first order of business is to e lect an acting chairperson who will 
conduct the meeting until such time as a president has been elected.  
Past practice has been that the previous year’s president conducts this 
part of the meeting. 
2. APPOINTMENT OF TEMPORARY SECRETARY 
 The second order of business is for the acting chairperson  to 
 appoint Judy Holmes to act as temporary secretary until such time as the 
 secretary to the Board has been elected. 
3. ELECTION OF PRESIDENT 
The acting chairperson declares nominations in order for the office o f 
president of the board of education (please note, nominations need not 
be seconded; and a motion and support are made to close the 
nomination.)  Please understand that four votes are required for election. 
4. ELECTION OF VICE PRESIDENT 
The newly elected  president declares nominations in order for the 
office of vice president, following the same procedures as outlined for the 
election of president. 
5. ELECTION OF A SECRETARY 
The president declares nominations in order for the office of secretary, 
following the same procedures as outlined for the election of president. 
6. APPOINTMENT OF TREASURER 
According to Board Policy, the treasurer is appointed annually by the 
Board as a nonvoting officer.  The assistant superintendent of business 
services has served  as treasurer of the Board.  It is my recommendation 
that this procedure continue.

         4. C.        01.15.19 
WHEREAS, there may be in and may, hereafter from time to time, come into the 
hands of Rick West , Treasurer of Troy School District, certain public monies 
belonging to or held for the State, County, or other political units of the State or 
otherwise held according to law, and 
WHEREAS, under the laws of Michigan, this Board is required to provide by 
Resolution for the deposit of all public monies, including tax monies coming into 
the hands of said Treasurer, on one or more banks, hereafter called bank(s) to be 
designated in such Resolution. 
NOW THEREFORE BE IT RESOLVED that said Treasurer, Rick West, is hereby 
directed to deposit and/or invest all public monies, including tax monies now in or 
coming into his hands as Treasurer in his name as Treasurer, into the  following 
depository institutions: 
Bank of America 
Bank of New York 
Charter One Bank 
JP Morgan Chase Bank 
Comerica Bank 
Huntington Bank  
Fifth Third Bank 
Flagstar Bancorp 
MILAF (Michigan Liquid Asset Fund Plus, managed 
By PFM Asset Management, Inc. through Fifth Third Bank) 
Morgan Stanley Smith Barney 
PFM Securities, Inc. through Fifth Third Bank 
PNC Bank 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED that monies belonging to the District may be 
withdrawn by checks signed with a facsimile signature of the Treasurer, Rick West, 
and in emergencies the handwritten signature of the Treasurer, Rick West, or the 
Superintendent, Richard M. Machesky, who may sign on the General Fund account 
and the Payroll account at Bank of America; and 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED that monies belonging to the District for the 
accounts established at PNC Bank  for purposes of paying Troy Career Center 
expenses may be withdrawn by checks with the handwritten signature of The Troy 
Career Center Manager, Assistant Manager, or Program Coordinators; and 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED that monies belonging to the District for the account 
established at Comerica Bank for purposes of paying health care and dependent 
care flexible spending claims may be withdrawn by ACH debit to Next Generation 
Enrollment, Inc., or by checks signed with a facsimile signature of the Treasurer, 
Rick West, and 
11
         4. C.        01.15.19 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED that the Treasurer, Rick West, may open or close 
accounts with the above -listed institutions, provided it is in the interest of the 
District; and 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED that funds of the District may be invested according 
to Board Policy 5000.06; and 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED that the Treasurer is authorized to combine money 
from more than one fund for the purpose of making an investment authorized by 
subsection (1) (h) of Section 380.1223 of the Revised School Code; and 
THE MAXIMUM AMOUNT TO BE INVESTED with the above institutions is 
regulated by the school code. 
I, ______________________, Secretary of Troy School District, Oakland County, 
Michigan, certify that the Resolution was adopted by said Board at a meeting 
thereof held on the date specified above. 
Given under my hand and seal of the Troy School District in the County of 
Oakland in the State of Michigan this 15th day of January 2019.

 5. A. 01.15.19 
5. RECOGNITION 
A. TSD Mission Moment – Baker Middle School Food Drive 
As this evening’s Mission Moment, we would like to recognize Baker Middle School 
for their efforts in hosting their second annual food drive.  The Food Drive was 
facilitated in Social Studies classrooms and run by a student force of 
approximately 50 students. All the food collected went to roughly 100 Troy 
Elementary and Middle School students who receive free and reduced lunch es. 
Due to the large quantit y collected this year, donations will be able to provide 
support for Baker and Morse families for several months! 
RECOMMENDED ACTION: Invite Jonathan Cross up to introduce those  
     involved.

      5. B.   01.15.19 
5.  RECOGNITION       
B.    Board of Education Recognition 
Each January, the Troy School District recognizes and celebrates the 
work of a very important group of individuals: The Board of Education.  
Our trustees work very hard to make the TSD a World Class district 
and provide students with amazing opportunities to learn and grow. 
This year, the Board will see the realization of a project that will offer 
even more children in Troy the opportunity for a high-quality education 
when we open our Troy School District Preschool this fall.  
So tonight, as a token of our appreciation for your tireless efforts on 
behalf of our students and staff, we present you with four tiles —or 
coasters—featuring elementary student art that will adorn the walls of 
our new preschool facility. These tiles represent nature, from our 
forests to our water, and symbolize the connection with our natural 
surroundings and the world around us that we intend to be a focus of 
our preschool programs. Through careful planning and the support of 
this Board, we know that our new Troy School District Preschool will 
truly be a place where exploration begins for our youngest learners. 
We hope that this small gift will serve as a reminder of our  gratitude 
to you, our Board trustees, for your support that will allow our 
preschool students to Explore, Create and Connect in a World Class 
setting. 
 Thank you for all you do.   
Recommended Action:  Present gift to Board members.

learning for all members of the school community; and 
WHEREAS, the Troy School Board of Education has adopted 
t h e  D i s t r i c t ’ s  s t r a t e g i c  p l a n  f o c u s i n g  t h e i r  w o r k  o n  f o u r  
characteristics of world class; and 
WHEREAS, one of the four characteristics is to  Invest in the 
Learning  of  Pre‐Kindergarten  Students  through  increasing 
access  to  a  high  quality,  literacy‐rich  preschool  experience; 
and 
WHEREAS, to increase access for a rich preschool experience, 
the  Troy  School  District  Board  of  Education  approved  the 
construction  of  a  new  Early  Childhood  Learning  Center  in 
December of 2017; and    
WHEREAS, the building is scheduled to open in the fall of 2019 
and Board Policy 6000.09 states the selection of future school 
names will be left to the discretion of the Board, 
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED THAT:   The Troy School 
District  Board  of  Education  hereby  names  the  new  building 
the Troy School District Preschool.

Dr.  Philippart  wished  everyone  happy  holidays  and  shared 
panel.  She also thanked the student reps for their reports to 
the Board and wished everyone happy holidays.

and also shared her feelings of Mr. Miletti’s leadership on the 
Board and how she enjoyed working with him.  
Mr. Hauff also shared his feelings on working with Mr. Miletti 
and  thanked  him  for  his  time  on  the  Board.    He  also 
mentioned  the  TFEE  Book  Fair  at  Barnes  and  Noble  he 
attended.   
Mr.  Gottlieb  echoed  the  other  Board  members’  comments.  
He also mentioned the Engagement Review that was held and 
the  positive  comments  received  and  acknowledge  the 
administration for the work they had done for this process.

that has been happening throughout the district and thanked 
Mr. Miletti for his service to the Troy School District.  He al so 
mentioned  about  being  in  constant  contact  with  our 
legislators  during  Lame  Duck  and  thanked  Representative 
Howrylak for opposing and holding up the bill on the proposed 
A‐F Grading System for Schools and talked about government 
funding.  He wished everyone happy holidays.

Board  and  wished  everyone  a  happy  and  restful  break.    He 
welcomed Dr. Nicole Wilson to the Board who will begin her 
duties in January.

High School & the Facility Operations Building.  The scope of work for this RFP is to provide 
complete roof replacement and roof restoration to roof areas at the specified schools that 
were identified as priorities through our Roof Asset Management Plan.

qualified bidder for the following schools:

Smith Middle – Roof Area E (Partial) 
Quality Roofing – Total Recommended Award $197,421.00 
Athens HS – Roof Area A (Sec. 4, 5, 8 & 10)

proposals for thoroughness and accuracy.  JD Candler and Royal West have both 
successfully performed similar work for the Troy School Distric t.  Quality Roofing has 
performed similar work for neighboring school districts. The Contractor Bid 
Recommendation by Weather Tech and Bid Summary is attached for complete bid results 
and support of this recommendation.

requested 10% Contingency. A separate Purchase Order will be issued for J.D. Candler , 
Royal West & Quality Roofing. The funding for these purchases will be from the Capit al 
Projects Fund.

the removal & installation of a new gym floor at Larson Middle School.

tabulation).  Conventional Carpet has successfully performed similar work for the Troy 
School District and Kuhn Specialty Flooring has performed similar work for other school 
districts in the Metro Detroit Area.

bids ($942,098.03), 10% Contingency for the Larson Gym Floor Portion ($11,250.00) 
and $15,000.00 for additional or emergency flooring replacement work as needed but 
not identified at bid time  (unit pricing was obtained through this bid for this specific 
purpose), a separate purchase order will be created for each site project.   
The funding for this purchase will be from Building Site & Sinking Project Funds.  The 
project is scheduled to commence on March 29th of 2019 although the majority of the 
work will not begin until June 14th, 2019 with a completion date of August 10th, 2019 
for all classroom portions, all other areas will be completed as soon as possible or as 
directed by Operations Management to best support all building s and scheduled 
activities.

Memorandum 
  TO: Dr. Rich Machesky, Superintendent of Schools 
Office 
4400 Livernois 
Troy, MI 48098 
Fax: 248.823.4013 
  RE: Policy Manual Updates / First Reading  
   DATE: Thursday, January 9, 2019 
We have reviewed and the following policy additions/changes with the Board Policy Committee and  
are recommending for First Reading at the January 15, 2019 Board of Education Meeting:   
1. We recommend adding the following underlined language to the second 
paragraph “Superintendent,” typically found under “Introduction” or Section 
0000.02, to resolve any confusion over when the Board may be represented by a 
negotiating team: 
The Board is represented in all labor negotiation proceedings by a negotiating 
team. The Board approves all members of the team prior to the start of the 
negotiations. All agreements negotiated by the team are subject to ratification by 
the Board. 
2. Federal law requires all school districts to implement a policy regarding the 
administration of physical examinations and screenings to students on school 
grounds.  Thus, we recommend adding this policy to the end of Section 2000.03 
as a stand -alone policy.  Also, please remember to update your annual 
notification documents to include physical examinations and screenings, as well 
as a corresponding opt-out form, if necessary:   
Physical Examinations and Screenings.  Annual notice will be given to parents 
of any health or physical examinations or screenings.  Parents will be given the 
opportunity to opt -out their students from all physical examinations and 
screenings. 
3. “Cardiac Emergency Response Plan” is referenced twice in the policy manual, 
typically under Sections 2000.07 and 8000.05.  Thus, we recommend replacing 
reference to “Cardiac Emergency Response Plan” under Section 2007/2000.07 
with the language under Section 8000.05 and removing Section 8000.05 from 
the policy manual.   
173
  FROM: Rick West, Assistant Superintendent, Business Services
  CC: Judy Holmes 
Business 
Phone: 248.823.4022 
4. A number of school districts have recently been audited by the Michigan State 
Police regarding their criminal background check policies and procedures.  
While we respectfully disagree with the Michigan State Police’s conclusion that 
additional information above and beyond what is already contained in our 
policies and regulations is required under the law, in the spirit of cooperation the 
following additions will satisfy the Michigan State Police’s audit.    
For our policies, we recommend addition of the following language to the end 
of “Criminal Background Checks” in Section 4000.02: 
The Superintendent will develop and implement administrative regulations to 
carry out this policy. 
5. The Michigan Revised School Code requires districts to have a policy 
prohibiting school district personnel from referring or assisting a student in 
obtaining an abortion.  The following policy is typically included under Section 
4000.05: 
Prohibition of Referral or Assistance:   All District employees, Board 
members, and school officials must provide instruction in sex education in strict 
compliance with MCL 380.1507 of the Revised School Code.  In addition, all 
District employees Board members, and school officials are prohibit ed from 
referring a student for an abortion or assisting a student in obtaining an abortion.    
Pursuant to state law, District personnel found to be in violation of this policy 
will be assessed a fine by the District of no less than 3% of his or her annua l 
compensation.  The District will refund the State School Aid Fund in an amount 
equal to any fines applied to or collected from District personnel under this 
policy. 
6. Per the United States Department of Education, all public school district policies 
and p rocedures manuals must include the following “Meal Charge” policy, 
which will be noted as Section 5000.10: 
Meal Charge/Food Services  
The School District has established this meal charge and food services policy to 
address the provision of food services for School District students, including:    
students who are eligible for reduced-price or full-price meal benefits, but and/or 
students who have insufficient funds to pay for school meals.  This policy also 
addresses bad debt incurred due to the School Dis trict’s inability to collect 
unpaid meal charges from its parents and students.    
The Superintendent will develop and implement administrative regulations 
regarding meal charge procedures to provide consistent direction concerning 
students who do not have funds in their account or in hand to cover the cost of 
their meal at the time of service, and procedures for students entitled to reduced-
price or full -price meal benefits.  These regulations, and this policy, will be 
provided to all households in writing  at the start of each school year, to 
households transferring to the School District during the school year, and to all 
School District staff and independent contractors and companies responsible for 
implementation. 
174
7. “Tobacco-Free Environment” and “Electr onic Cigarettes, Vaporizers, Etc.,” 
typically found under Section 6000.05 and 6000.06 , respectively, both 
prohibited the use of tobacco and tobacco -related products on school grounds.  
Thus, we recommend removing Section 6006.06 in its entirety and combini ng 
both policies under Section 6000.05, as follows: 
Generally  The use of all tobacco products on School District property is 
prohibited.  For purposes of this policy, “School District Property” includes all 
school buildings, areas adjacent to school buil dings, athletic fields, pupil 
transportation vehicles and parking lots.  This prohibition applies to students, 
employees and visitors to the school or school campus and applies whether or 
not school is in session.  The term “tobacco” includes any kind of l ighted pipe, 
cigar, cigarettes, or any other lighted smoking materials, as well as chewing 
products and snuff.  
Electronic Cigarettes, Vaporizers, Etc.  The use of electronic cigarettes, 
vaporizers, etc. on School District property is prohibited.    
For purposes of this policy, "Electronic Cigarettes" and “Vaporizers” means any 
device that simulates smoking any type of product, regardless whether they are 
manufactured, distributed, marketed or sold as e-cigarettes, or under any product 
name or descriptor. 
8. Many districts have expressed interest in a policy regarding district support 
organizations, such as parent-teacher associations, student fundraising activities, 
and so forth.  To that end, we recommend addition of the “District Support 
Organizations” policy, typically noted as Section 7000.07: 
District Support Organizations  
The Board recognizes the important role that supportive members of the 
community can make in creating educational opportunities for District students.  
To that end, the Board encourages the creation of District support organizations, 
including student fundraising activities, booster clubs, parent/teacher 
organizations, etc., to provide additional support to the School District 
community.  The Superintendent will develop and imp lement administrative 
regulations to carry out this policy. 
9. Many school districts have expressed interest in a policy regarding digital 
communications made by its staff and students, both while on school grounds 
and at home.  To that end, we recommend ad dition of “Digital 
Communications,” typically noted as Section 8000.10: 
Digital Communications  
Digital communication (including social networking) that occurs on district 
premises or involves the use of district equipment is governed by the Acceptable 
Use Policy and this Policy.  This Policy also applies to digital communication 
that occurs off District premises and/or using non-District equipment. 
Digital communication (including social networking) provides educational and 
other opportunities for staff and students.  The Board of Education expects that 
staff and students who engage in digital communication will do so in a 
reasonable and appropriate manner.  Specifically, digital communication 
between staff and students, or to which students reasonably may  be exposed, 
175
should be professional and of the same content, tone and demeanor as in-school 
communication between staff and students.  Similarly, digital communication 
between staff and parents, community members and other adults, or to  which 
staff members, parents and community members reasonably may be exposed, 
should be professional.
